&=\sum_{n=N+1}^{M}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{(n+1,...,M)\underline{\mathbf{b}}}{}^{r}\mathcal{Q}^{(k)}_{n,\underline{\mathbf{b}},(n+1,...,K)}\mathcal{R}_{(n+1,...,M)\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\\
&\quad+\sum_{n=1}^{N}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{(n+1,...,M)\underline{\mathbf{b}}}{}^{r}\mathcal{Q}^{(k)}_{n,\underline{\mathbf{b}},(n+1,...,K)}\mathcal{R}_{(n+1,...,M)\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\\
&=\gamma_{k}^{(1)}(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{N+1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{M},\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{M+1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{K})\\
&\quad+\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{(N+1,...,M)\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{(n+1,...,N)\underline{\mathbf{b}}}{}^{r}\mathcal{Q}^{(k)}_{n,\underline{\mathbf{b}},(n+1,...,K)}\mathcal{R}_{(n+1,...,N)\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\mathcal{R}_{(N+1,...,M)\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\\
&=\gamma_{k}^{(1)}(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{N+1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{M},\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{M+1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{K})\\
&\quad+\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{(N+1,...,M)\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\gamma^{(1)}_{k}(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{N},\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{N+1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{K})\mathcal{R}_{(N+1,...,M)\underline{\mathbf{b}}},\end{split}

which proves the first equality.

For the second equality, let
N>0
and
M>N+k-3
. Using Lemma
A.1
, we get

\begin{split}&\gamma^{(1)}_{k}(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{N},\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{N+1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{M})\\
&=\sum_{n=1}^{N}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{(n+1,...,N)(\underline{\mathbf{b}}\underline{\mathbf{c}})}{}^{r}\mathcal{Q}^{(k)}_{n,(\underline{\mathbf{b}\mathbf{c}}),(n+1,...,M)}\mathcal{R}_{(n+1,...,N)(\underline{\mathbf{b}\mathbf{c}})}\\
&=\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{(N+1,...,M)\underline{\mathbf{c}}}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{(n+1,...,N)\underline{\mathbf{b}}}{}^{r}\mathcal{Q}^{(k)}_{n,\underline{\mathbf{b}},(n+1,...,M)}\mathcal{R}_{(n+1,...,N)\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\mathcal{R}_{(N+1,...,M)\underline{\mathbf{c}}}\\
&\quad+\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{(1,...,N)\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{(n+1,...,N)\underline{\mathbf{c}}}{}^{r}\mathcal{Q}^{(k)}_{n,\underline{\mathbf{c}},(n+1,...,M)}\mathcal{R}_{(n+1,...,N)\underline{\mathbf{c}}}\mathcal{R}_{(1,...,N)\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\\
&=\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{(N+1,...,M)\underline{\mathbf{c}}}\gamma^{(1)}_{k}(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{N},\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{N+1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{M})\mathcal{R}_{(N+1,...,M)\underline{\mathbf{c}}}\\
&\quad+\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{(1,...,N)\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\gamma^{(1)}_{k}(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}}\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{N},\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{N+1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{M})\mathcal{R}_{(1,...,N)\underline{\mathbf{b}}}.\end{split}

(152)

It can be readily verified that the equality still holds under the substitution
\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\to 1
and for all
0<N<M
, thus proving the second equality.

For the last equality, let
N>0
,
M>N+k-3
and
K>M
. Using Lemma
2.9
, it immediately follows that

\begin{split}&\gamma^{(1)}_{k}(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{N},\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{K})\\
&=\sum_{n=1}^{N}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{(n+1,...,N)\underline{\mathbf{b}}}{}^{r}\mathcal{Q}^{(k)}_{n,\underline{\mathbf{b}},(n+1,...,K)}\mathcal{R}_{(n+1,...,N)\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\\
&=\sum_{n=1}^{N}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{(n+1,...,N)\underline{\mathbf{b}}}{}^{r}\mathcal{Q}^{(k)}_{n,\underline{\mathbf{b}},(n+1,...,M)}\mathcal{R}_{(n+1,...,N)\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\\
&=\gamma^{(1)}_{k}(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{N},\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{M})\varepsilon(\mathcal{T}_{M+1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{K}).\end{split}

Note that this equality also holds trivially true in the case
\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\to 1
with arbitrary
M>N
and
K>M
. Thus, we prove the last equality and therefore the lemma.
∎

We can now prove Proposition
3.4
:

Proof of Proposition
3.4
.

Let
a\otimes b,c\otimes d,e\otimes f\in\mathcal{A}_{0}\oplus\left(\mathcal{A}^{(\geq 1)}(\mathcal{R})\bowtie_{\mathbf{r}}\mathcal{A}_{0}^{(\geq k-2)}\right)
, then

\begin{split}\phi^{(1)}_{k}(a\otimes b,c\otimes d,e\otimes f)&=\gamma^{(1)}_{k}(c\otimes d,e\otimes f)\varepsilon(a)\varepsilon(b)+\gamma^{(1)}_{k}(a\otimes b,(c\otimes d)(e\otimes f))\\
&\quad-\gamma^{(1)}_{k}((a\otimes b)(c\otimes d),e\otimes f)-\gamma^{(1)}_{k}(a\otimes b,c\otimes d)\varepsilon(e)\varepsilon(f).\end{split}

(153)

From Lemma
A.3
, it now follows that

\begin{split}&\gamma^{(1)}_{k}(a\otimes b,(c\otimes d)(e\otimes f))=\sum\mathbf{r}^{-1}(d_{(1)},e_{(1)})\gamma^{(1)}_{k}(a\otimes b,c\cdot e_{(2)}\otimes d_{(2)}\cdot f)\mathbf{r}(d_{(3)},e_{(3)})\\
&=\sum\mathbf{r}^{-1}(d_{(1)},e_{(1)})\mathbf{r}^{-1}(b_{(1)},c_{(1)})\gamma^{(1)}_{k}(a\cdot c_{(2)}\otimes b_{(2)},e_{(2)}\otimes d_{(2)}\cdot f)\mathbf{r}(b_{(3)},c_{(3)})\mathbf{r}(d_{(3)},e_{(3)})\\
&\quad+\sum\mathbf{r}^{-1}(e_{(1)},f_{(1)})\gamma^{(1)}_{k}(a\otimes b,c\otimes d\cdot f_{(2)})\mathbf{r}(e_{(2)},f_{(3)})\\
&=\sum\mathbf{r}^{-1}(d_{(1)},e_{(1)})\mathbf{r}^{-1}(b_{(1)},c_{(1)})\gamma^{(1)}_{k}(a\cdot c_{(2)}\otimes b_{(2)},e_{(2)}\otimes d_{(2)}\cdot f)\mathbf{r}(b_{(3)},c_{(3)})\mathbf{r}(d_{(3)},e_{(3)})\\
&\quad+\gamma^{(1)}_{k}(a\otimes b,c\otimes d)\varepsilon(e)\varepsilon(f),\end{split}

(154)

where in the second equality we used that
\sum\mathbf{r}(e_{(1)},d_{(1)})\mathbf{r}(d_{(2)},e_{(2)})=\varepsilon(e)\varepsilon(d)
since
\mathbf{r}
is cotriangular. Similarly, we have

\begin{split}&\gamma^{(1)}_{k}((a\otimes b)(c\otimes d),e\otimes f)=\sum\mathbf{r}^{-1}(b_{(1)},c_{(1)})\gamma_{k}^{(1)}(a\cdot c_{(2)}\otimes b_{(2)}\cdot d,e\otimes f)\mathbf{r}(b_{(3)},c_{(3)})\\
&=\sum\mathbf{r}^{-1}(b_{(1)},c_{(1)})\mathbf{r}^{-1}(d_{(1)},e_{(1)})\gamma_{k}^{(1)}(a\cdot c_{(2)}\otimes b_{(2)},e_{(2)}\otimes d_{(2)}\cdot f)\mathbf{r}(d_{(3)},e_{(3)})\mathbf{r}(b_{(3)},c_{(3)})\\
&\quad+\gamma_{k}^{(1)}(a\cdot c\otimes d,e\otimes f)\varepsilon(b).\end{split}

(155)

By combining all the terms, and using the fact that
\gamma^{(1)}_{k}(a\cdot c\otimes d,e\otimes f)=\gamma^{(1)}_{k}(c\otimes d,e\otimes f)\varepsilon(a)
, we find that indeed the Drinfeld associator vanishes, i.e.
\phi^{(1)}_{k}(a\otimes b,c\otimes d,e\otimes f)=0
, thus proving the proposition.
∎

A.4
Proofs for the bilocal deformation

This section will be devoted to proving Proposition
3.5
. In order to break up the proof in more digestible parts, we will write the twist
\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}
as given in (
104
) into the three parts

\begin{split}&{}^{1}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{N},(1\otimes\mathcal{T}_{N+1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{M})(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\otimes 1))\\
&\coloneq\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,N+\ell-2)}{}^{r}\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{(k)}_{(1,...,N+\ell-2),\underline{\mathbf{a}},N+\ell-1}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,N+\ell-2)}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(N+3-\ell,...,M)}{}^{r}\mathcal{Q}^{(\ell)}_{N+2-\ell,\underline{\mathbf{b}},(N+3-\ell,...M)}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(N+3-\ell,...,M)}\\
&+\sum_{n=N+2,}^{N+\ell-1}\sum_{m=N+2-\ell}^{n-\ell}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,M)}[\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}^{(k)}_{n-k+1,...,n},\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,M)}][\mathfrak{q}^{(\ell)}_{m,...,m+\ell-1},\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(1,...,M)}]\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(1,...,M)},\\
&{}^{2}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{N},(1\otimes\mathcal{T}_{N+1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{M})(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\otimes 1))\\
&\coloneq\sum_{n=1}^{N}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,M)}\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}^{(k)}_{n+\ell-k,...,n+\ell-1}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,....M)}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(n+1,...,M)}{}^{r}\mathcal{Q}^{(\ell)}_{n,\underline{\mathbf{b}},(n+1,...,M)}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(n+1,...,M)}^{-1},\\
&{}^{3}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{N},(1\otimes\mathcal{T}_{N+1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{M})(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\otimes 1))\\
&\coloneq\sum_{\mathclap{n=N+1}}^{M}\ \mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,n-1)}{}^{r}\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{(k)}_{(1,...,n-1),\underline{\mathbf{a}},n}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,n-1)}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(1,...,M)}\mathfrak{q}^{(\ell)}_{n-\ell+1,...,n}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(1,...,M)},\end{split}

(156)

such that
\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}={}^{1}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}+{}^{2}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}+{}^{3}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}
. We now have the following intermediate result:

A.4 Lemma
.

For
2\leq k<\ell
, the twist elements
{}^{2,3}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}
as defined in (
156
) satisfy the following relations:

•

For
a,c,c^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{R})
,
b,b^{\prime},d,d^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}
, such that
c\otimes d\in\mathcal{A}_{0}\oplus\left(\mathcal{A}^{(\geq 1)}(\mathcal{R})\bowtie_{\mathbf{r}}\mathcal{A}_{0}^{(\geq\ell-1)}\right)
:

\begin{split}{}^{2}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(a\otimes b,c\cdot c^{\prime}\otimes d)&=\sum\mathbf{r}^{-1}(c^{\prime}_{(1)},d_{(1)})\ {}^{2}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(a\otimes b,c\otimes d_{(2)})\mathbf{r}(c^{\prime}_{(2)},d_{(3)})\\
&\quad+\sum\mathbf{r}^{-1}(b_{(1)},c_{(1)})\ {}^{2}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(a\cdot c_{(2)}\otimes b_{(2)},c^{\prime}\otimes d)\mathbf{r}(b_{(3)},c_{(3)}),\\
{}^{2}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(a\otimes b,c\otimes d\cdot d^{\prime})&={}^{2}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(a\otimes b,c\otimes d)\varepsilon(d^{\prime}),\\
{}^{2}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(a\otimes b\cdot b^{\prime},c\otimes d)&=\sum\mathbf{r}^{-1}(b^{\prime}_{(1)},c_{(1)})\ {}^{2}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(a\otimes b,c_{(2)}\otimes b^{\prime}_{(2)}\cdot d)\mathbf{r}(b^{\prime}_{(3)},c_{(3)})\\
&\quad+\sum\mathbf{r}^{-1}(a_{(1)},b_{(1)})\ {}^{2}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(a_{(2)}\otimes b^{\prime},c\otimes d)\mathbf{r}(a_{(3)},b_{(2)}).\end{split}

•

For
a,a^{\prime},c\in\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{R})
,
b,b^{\prime},d,d^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{0}
such that
a\otimes b\in\mathcal{A}_{0}\oplus\left(\mathcal{A}^{(\geq 1)}(\mathcal{R})\bowtie_{\mathbf{r}}\mathcal{A}_{0}^{(\geq\ell-1)}\right)
:

\begin{split}{}^{3}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(a\cdot a^{\prime}\otimes b,c\otimes d)&=\varepsilon(a)\ {}^{3}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(a^{\prime}\otimes b,c\otimes d)\\
&\quad+\sum\mathbf{r}^{-1}(a^{\prime}_{(1)},b_{(1)})\ {}^{3}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(a\otimes b_{(2)},a^{\prime}_{(2)}\cdot c\otimes d)\mathbf{r}(a^{\prime}_{(3)},b_{(3)}),\\
{}^{3}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(a\otimes b\cdot b^{\prime},c\otimes d)&=\sum\mathbf{r}^{-1}(a_{(1)},b_{(1)})\ {}^{3}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(a_{(2)}\otimes b^{\prime},c\otimes d)\mathbf{r}(a_{(3)},b_{(2)}),\\
{}^{3}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(a\otimes b,c\otimes d\cdot d^{\prime})&={}^{3}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(a\otimes b,c\otimes d)\varepsilon(d^{\prime})\\
&\quad+\sum\mathbf{r}^{-1}(c_{(1)},d_{(1)})\ {}^{3}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(a\otimes b\cdot d_{(2)},c_{(2)}\otimes d^{\prime})\mathbf{r}(c_{(3)},d_{(3)}).\end{split}

Proof.

Let us first consider the equalities for
{}^{2}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}
. Let
N>0
and
M>N+\ell-2
, then

\begin{split}&{}^{2}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{N},(1\otimes\mathcal{T}_{N+1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{M})(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}}\otimes 1))\\
&=\sum_{n=1}^{N}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,M)}\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}^{(k)}_{n+\ell-k,...,n+\ell-1}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,....M)}\mathcal{R}_{(\underline{\mathbf{b}\mathbf{c})}(n+1,...,M)}{}^{r}\mathcal{Q}^{(\ell)}_{n,\underline{\mathbf{b}\mathbf{c}},(n+1,...,M)}\mathcal{R}_{(\underline{\mathbf{b}\mathbf{c}})(n+1,...,M)}^{-1}\\
&=\sum_{n=1}^{N}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,M)}\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}^{(k)}_{n+\ell-k,...,n+\ell-1}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,....M)}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(n+1,...,M)}{}^{r}\mathcal{Q}^{(\ell)}_{n,\underline{\mathbf{b}},(n+1,...,M)}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(n+1,...,M)}^{-1}\\
&+\sum_{n=1}^{N}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,M)}\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}^{(k)}_{n+\ell-k,...,n+\ell-1}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,....M)}\mathcal{R}_{(\underline{\mathbf{b}\mathbf{c}})(n+1,...,M)}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\mathcal{R}_{n\underline{\mathbf{b}}}^{-1}{}^{r}\mathcal{Q}^{(\ell)}_{n,\underline{\mathbf{c}},(n+1,...,M)}\mathcal{R}_{n\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\mathcal{R}_{(\underline{\mathbf{b}\mathbf{c}})(n+1,...,M)}^{-1},\end{split}

where we used Lemma
A.1
in the second equality. Using the fact that

[\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(1,...,n-1)},\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(n+1,....,M)}{}^{r}\mathcal{Q}^{(\ell)}_{n,\underline{\mathbf{c}},(n+1,...,M)}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(n+1,...,M)}]=0,

for all
n\leq N
, this then gives

\begin{split}&{}^{2}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{N},(1\otimes\mathcal{T}_{N+1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{M})(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}}\otimes 1))\\
&={}^{2}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{N},(1\otimes\mathcal{T}_{N+1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{M})(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\otimes 1))\\
&\quad+\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{(1,...,M)\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{(\underline{\mathbf{a}\mathbf{b}})(1,...,M)}\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}^{(k)}_{n+\ell-k,...,n+\ell-1}\mathcal{R}_{(\underline{\mathbf{a}\mathbf{b}})(1,...,M)}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(n+1,....,M)}{}^{r}\mathcal{Q}^{(\ell)}_{n,\underline{\mathbf{c}},(n+1,...,M)}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(n+1,...,M)}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(1,...,M)}\\
&={}^{2}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{N},(1\otimes\mathcal{T}_{N+1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{M})(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\otimes 1))\\
&\quad+\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{(1,...,M)\underline{\mathbf{b}}}{}^{2}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}}\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{N},(1\otimes\mathcal{T}_{N+1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{M})(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}}\otimes 1))\mathcal{R}_{(1,...,M)\underline{\mathbf{b}}},\end{split}

Note that the equality will still hold if we replace
\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}}\to 1
. Moreover, since the twist element satisfies
{}^{2}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(a\otimes b,1\otimes d)=0
, the result is also true under the substitution
\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\to 1
and/or
\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}}\to 1
for any
M>N
, thus proving the first equality.

For the second equality, let
N>0
,
M>N+\ell-2
and
K>M
, then

\begin{split}&{}^{2}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{N},(1\otimes\mathcal{T}_{N+1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{K})(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\otimes 1))\\
&=\sum_{n=1}^{N}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,K)}\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}^{(k)}_{n+\ell-k,...,n+\ell-1}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,....K)}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(n+1,...,K)}{}^{r}\mathcal{Q}^{(\ell)}_{n,\underline{\mathbf{b}},(n+1,...,K)}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(n+1,...,K)}^{-1}\\
&=\sum_{n=1}^{N}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,M)}\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}^{(k)}_{n+\ell-k,...,n+\ell-1}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,....M)}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(M+1,...,K)}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(n+1,...,M)}{}^{r}\mathcal{Q}^{(\ell)}_{n,\underline{\mathbf{b}},(n+1,...,M)}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(n+1,...,M)}^{-1}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(M+1,...,K)}\\
&=\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{(M+1,...,K)\underline{\mathbf{b}}}{}^{2}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{N},(1\otimes\mathcal{T}_{N+1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{M})(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\otimes 1))\mathcal{R}_{(M+1,...,K)\underline{\mathbf{b}}},\end{split}

(157)

where in the second equality we used the fact that
n+\ell-1<N+\ell-1<M+1
such that

\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,K)}\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}^{(k)}_{n+\ell-k,...,n+\ell-1}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,....K)}=\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,M)}\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}^{(k)}_{n+\ell-k,...,n+\ell-1}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,....M)},

(158)

and moreover, we could pull the term
\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(M+1,...,K)}
to the left from the second to the third equality in (
157
). Note that under the substitution
\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\to 1
, the twist element
{}^{3}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}
in (
157
) vanishes, such that the result in that case holds for any
K>M>N>0
, thus proving the second equality for
{}^{3}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}
.

Lastly, let
0<N<M
and
K>M+\ell-2
, then

\begin{split}&{}^{2}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{M},(1\otimes\mathcal{T}_{M+1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{K})(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\otimes 1))\\
&=\sum_{n=1}^{N}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,K)}\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}^{(k)}_{n+\ell-k,...,n+\ell-1}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,....K)}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(n+1,...,M)}{}^{r}\mathcal{Q}^{(\ell)}_{n,\underline{\mathbf{b}},(n+1,...,K)}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(n+1,...,K)}^{-1}\\
&\quad+\sum_{n=N+1}^{M}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,K)}\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}^{(k)}_{n+\ell-k,...,n+\ell-1}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,....K)}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(n+1,...,M)}{}^{r}\mathcal{Q}^{(\ell)}_{n,\underline{\mathbf{b}},(n+1,...,K)}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(n+1,...,K)}^{-1}\\
&={}^{2}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{N},(1\otimes\mathcal{T}_{M+1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{K})(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\otimes 1))\\
&\quad+\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,N)}{}^{2}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{N+1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{M},(1\otimes\mathcal{T}_{M+1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{K})(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\otimes 1))\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,N)}.\end{split}

(159)

Again, note here that
{}^{2}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}
vanishes under substitution
\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\to 1
, such that the above equality in that case also holds for any
K>M
. This then proves the last equality for
{}^{2}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}
.

All the equalities for
{}^{3}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}
will follow in a completely analogous way to the equalities
{}^{2}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}
, which then proves the statement.
∎

Secondly, we consider the following intermediate result:

A.5 Lemma
.

For
2\leq k<\ell
and
a\otimes b,c\otimes d,e\otimes f\in\mathcal{A}_{0}\oplus\left(\mathcal{A}^{(\geq 1)}(\mathcal{R})\bowtie_{\mathbf{r}}\mathcal{A}_{0}^{(\geq\ell-1)}\right)
, we have

\begin{split}&\varepsilon(a\cdot b)\ {}^{2}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(c\otimes d,e\otimes f)-\sum\mathbf{r}^{-1}(a_{(1)},b_{(1)})\ {}^{2}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(a_{(2)}\cdot c\otimes d,e\otimes f)\mathbf{r}(a_{(3)},b_{(2)})\\
&+\sum\mathbf{r}^{-1}(d_{(1)},e_{(1)})\ {}^{3}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(a\otimes b,c\cdot e_{(2)}\otimes d_{(2)})\varepsilon(f)\mathbf{r}(d_{(3)},e_{(3)})-{}^{3}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(a\otimes b,c\otimes d)\varepsilon(e\cdot f)\\
&=\sum\mathbf{r}^{-1}(b_{(1)},c_{(1)})\ {}^{1}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(a\cdot c_{(2)}\otimes b_{(2)}d,e\otimes f)\mathbf{r}(b_{(3)},c_{(3)})-\varepsilon(a\cdot b)\ {}^{1}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(c\otimes d,e\otimes f)\\
&+\sum{}^{1}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(a\otimes b,c\otimes d)\varepsilon(e\cdot f)-\mathbf{r}^{-1}(d_{(1)},e_{(1)})\ {}^{1}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(a\otimes b,ce_{(2)}\otimes d_{(2)})\varepsilon(f)\mathbf{r}(d_{(3)},e_{(3)}).\end{split}

Proof.

Let
N>\ell-2
,
M>N+\ell-2
and
K>M+\ell-2
, then

\begin{split}&{}^{2}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}}\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{N+1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{M},(1\otimes\mathcal{T}_{M+1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{K})(1\otimes\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}}))\\
&=\sum_{\mathclap{n=N+1}}^{M}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{(\underline{\mathbf{a}\mathbf{b}})(N+1,...,K)}\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}^{(k)}_{n+\ell-k,...,n+\ell-1}\mathcal{R}_{(\underline{\mathbf{a}\mathbf{b}})(N+1,....K)}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(n+1,...,K)}{}^{r}\mathcal{Q}^{(\ell)}_{n,\underline{\mathbf{c}},(n+1,...,K)}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(n+1,...,K)}^{-1}\\
&=\sum_{\mathclap{n=N+1}}^{M}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(N+1,...,K)}\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}^{(k)}_{n+\ell-k,...,n+\ell-1}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(N+1,....K)}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(n+1,...,K)}{}^{r}\mathcal{Q}^{(\ell)}_{n,\underline{\mathbf{c}},(n+1,...,K)}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(n+1,...,K)}^{-1}\\
&\quad+\sum_{\mathclap{n=N+1}}^{M}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(N+1,...,K)}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(N+1,...,K)}[\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}^{(k)}_{n+\ell-k,...,n+\ell-1},\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(N+1,...,K)}]\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(N+1,....K)}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(n+1,...,K)}{}^{r}\mathcal{Q}^{(\ell)}_{n,\underline{\mathbf{c}},(n+1,...,K)}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(n+1,...,K)}^{-1}.\end{split}

From the Sutherland equations as given in Proposition
2.11
, it follows that

\begin{split}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,K)}[\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}^{(k)}_{n+\ell-k,...,n+\ell-1},\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,K)}]&=\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,n+\ell-3)}{}^{r}\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{(k)}_{(1,...,n+\ell-3),\underline{\mathbf{a}},n+\ell-2}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,n+\ell-3)}\\
&-\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,n+\ell-2)}{}^{r}\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{(k)}_{(1,...,n+\ell-2),\underline{\mathbf{a}},n+\ell-1}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,n+\ell-2)}.\end{split}

Combining these results, one finds that

\begin{split}&{}^{2}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{N+1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{M},\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{M+1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{K})\\
&\quad-\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,N)}{}^{2}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}}\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{N+1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{M},\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{M+1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{K})\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,N)}\\
&=\sum_{\mathclap{n=N+1}}^{M}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(M+1,...,K)}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(N+1,...,K)}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,n+\ell-2)}{}^{r}\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{(k)}_{(1,...,n+\ell-2),\underline{\mathbf{a}},n+\ell-1}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,n+\ell-2)}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(N+1,....K)}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(n+1,...,K)}{}^{r}\mathcal{Q}^{(\ell)}_{n,\underline{\mathbf{c}},(n+1,...,K)}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(n+1,...,M)}^{-1}\\
&-\sum_{\mathclap{n=N+1}}^{M}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(M+1,...,K)}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(N+1,...,K)}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,n+\ell-3)}{}^{r}\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{(k)}_{(1,...,n+\ell-3),\underline{\mathbf{a}},n+\ell-2}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,n+\ell-3)}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(N+1,....K)}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(n+1,...,K)}{}^{r}\mathcal{Q}^{(\ell)}_{n,\underline{\mathbf{c}},(n+1,...,K)}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(n+1,...,M)}^{-1}.\end{split}

(160)

In an analogous way, it can be found that

\begin{split}&\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{(N+1,...,M)\underline{\mathbf{c}}}{}^{3}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{N},\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{N+1,...,M})\mathcal{R}_{(N+1,...,M)\underline{\mathbf{c}}}\\
&\quad-{}^{3}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{N},\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{N+1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{M})\\
&=\sum_{n=N+1}^{M}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(N+1,...,M)}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,n-1)}{}^{r}\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{(k)}_{(1,...,n-1),\underline{\mathbf{a}},n}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,n-1)}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(1,...,M)}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(n-\ell+3,...,M)}{}^{r}\mathcal{Q}^{(\ell)}_{n-\ell+2,\underline{\mathbf{c}},(n-\ell+3,...,M)}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(n-\ell+3,...,M)}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(1,...,N)}\\
&-\sum_{n=N+1}^{M}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(N+1,...,M)}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,n-1)}{}^{r}\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{(k)}_{(1,...,n-1),\underline{\mathbf{a}},n}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,n-1)}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(1,...,M)}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(n-\ell+2,...,M)}{}^{r}\mathcal{Q}^{(\ell)}_{n-\ell+1,\underline{\mathbf{c}},(n-\ell+2,...,M)}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(n-\ell+2,...,M)}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(1,...,N)}.\end{split}

(161)

Importantly, if we would replace
n\to n+\ell-2
and
n\to n+\ell-1
in the first and second sum of (
161
), respectively, then these terms cancel most of the terms in the sum in (
160
) up to some boundary terms if we sum up (
160
) and (
161
). In particular, we find that

\begin{split}&{}^{2}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{N+1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{M},\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{M+1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{K})\\
&\quad-\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,N)}{}^{2}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}}\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{N+1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{M},\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{M+1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{K})\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,N)}\\
&+\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{(N+1,...,M)\underline{\mathbf{c}}}{}^{3}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{N},\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{N+1,...,M})\mathcal{R}_{(N+1,...,M)\underline{\mathbf{c}}}\\
&\quad-{}^{3}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{N},\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{N+1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{M})\\
&=A+B,\end{split}

where

\begin{split}A&=\sum_{\mathclap{n=M+2-\ell}}^{M}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(M+1,...,K)}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(N+1,...,K)}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,n+\ell-2)}{}^{r}\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{(k)}_{(1,...,n+\ell-2),\underline{\mathbf{a}},n+\ell-1}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,n+\ell-2)}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(N+1,....K)}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(n+1,...,K)}{}^{r}\mathcal{Q}^{(\ell)}_{n,\underline{\mathbf{c}},(n+1,...,K)}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(n+1,...,M)}^{-1}\\
&-\sum_{\mathclap{n=M+3-\ell}}^{M}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(M+1,...,K)}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(N+1,...,K)}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,n+\ell-3)}{}^{r}\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{(k)}_{(1,...,n+\ell-3),\underline{\mathbf{a}},n+\ell-2}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,n+\ell-3)}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(N+1,....K)}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(n+1,...,K)}{}^{r}\mathcal{Q}^{(\ell)}_{n,\underline{\mathbf{c}},(n+1,...,K)}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(n+1,...,M)}^{-1}\end{split}

and

\begin{split}B&=\sum_{n=N+1}^{N+\ell-2}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(N+1,...,M)}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,n-1)}{}^{r}\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{(k)}_{(1,...,n-1),\underline{\mathbf{a}},n}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,n-1)}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(1,...,M)}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(n-\ell+3,...,M)}{}^{r}\mathcal{Q}^{(\ell)}_{n-\ell+2,\underline{\mathbf{c}},(n-\ell+3,...,M)}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(n-\ell+3,...,M)}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(1,...,N)}\\
&-\sum_{n=N+1}^{N+\ell-1}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(N+1,...,M)}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,n-1)}{}^{r}\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{(k)}_{(1,...,n-1),\underline{\mathbf{a}},n}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,n-1)}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(1,...,M)}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(n-\ell+2,...,M)}{}^{r}\mathcal{Q}^{(\ell)}_{n-\ell+1,\underline{\mathbf{c}},(n-\ell+2,...,M)}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(n-\ell+2,...,M)}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(1,...,N)}.\end{split}

Note here that
A
and
B
now contain sums over only
\ell-1
terms. Let us evaluate
A
. Firstly, it can be seen that the two sums in the domain
n=M+3-\ell
to
M
can be combined, in which one can recognise the Sutherland equation as given in Proposition
2.11
. In particular, this gives

\begin{split}A&=\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(M+1,...,K)}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(N+1,...,K)}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,M)}{}^{r}\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{(k)}_{(1,...,M),\underline{\mathbf{a}},M+1}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,M)}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(N+1,...,K)}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(M+3-\ell,...,K)}{}^{r}\mathcal{Q}^{(\ell)}_{M+2-\ell,\underline{\mathbf{c}},(M+3-\ell,...,K)}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(M+3-\ell,...,M)}\\
&+\sum_{n=M+3-\ell}^{M}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(M+1,...,K)}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(N+1,...,K)}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,K)}[\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...K)},\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}^{(k)}_{n+\ell-k,...,n+\ell-1}]\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(N+1,....K)}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(n+1,...,K)}{}^{r}\mathcal{Q}^{(\ell)}_{n,\underline{\mathbf{c}},(n+1,...,K)}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(n+1,...,M)}^{-1}\end{split}

Next, by repeated application of the Sutherland equations as given in Proposition
2.11
, it follows that

\begin{split}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,M)}{}^{r}\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{(k)}_{(1,...,M),\underline{\mathbf{a}},M+1}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,M)}&=\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,M+\ell-2)}{}^{r}\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{(k)}_{(1,...,M+\ell-2),\underline{\mathbf{a}},M+\ell-1}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,M+\ell-2)}\\
&+\sum_{n=M+3-\ell}^{M}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,K)}[\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}^{(k)}_{n+\ell-k,...,n+\ell-1},\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,K)}],\end{split}

and similarly, for
n\geq M+3-\ell
,

\begin{split}&\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(M+3-\ell,...,K)}{}^{r}\mathcal{Q}^{(\ell)}_{M+2-\ell,\underline{\mathbf{c}},(M+3-\ell,...,K)}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(M+3-\ell,...,K)}\\
&=\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(n+1,...,K)}{}^{r}\mathcal{Q}^{(\ell)}_{n,\underline{\mathbf{c}},(n+1,...,K)}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(n+1,...,K)}+\sum_{m=M+2-\ell}^{n-1}[\mathfrak{q}^{(\ell)}_{m,...,m+\ell-1},\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(1,...,K)}]\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(1,...,K)}.\end{split}

Combining these facts then gives

\begin{split}A&=\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(M+1,...,K)}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(N+1,...,K)}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,M+\ell-2)}{}^{r}\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{(k)}_{(1,...,M+\ell-2),\underline{\mathbf{a}},M+\ell-1}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,M+\ell-2)}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(N+1,...,K)}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(M+3-\ell,...,K)}{}^{r}\mathcal{Q}^{(\ell)}_{M+2-\ell,\underline{\mathbf{c}},(M+3-\ell,...,K)}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(M+3-\ell,...,M)}\\
&+\sum_{n=M+3-\ell}^{M}\sum_{\ m=M+2-\ell}^{n-1}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(M+1,...,K)}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(N+1,...,K)}\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...,K)}[\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}^{(k)}_{n+\ell-k,...,n+\ell-1},\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(1,...K)}]\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}(N+1,....K)}[\mathfrak{q}^{(\ell)}_{m,...,m+\ell-1},\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(1,...,K)}]\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}(1,...,M)}.\end{split}

Using Lemma
A.1
, it then immediately follows from the definitions of the twist element
{}^{1}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}
as given in (
156
) that

\begin{split}A&=\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{(1,...,N)\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\ {}^{1}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}}\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{M},\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{M+1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{K})\mathcal{R}_{(1,...,N)\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\\
&\qquad\qquad-{}^{1}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{N+1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{M},\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{M+1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{K})\big.\end{split}

In a completely analogous way, it can be shown that

\begin{split}B&={}^{1}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{N},\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{N+1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{M})\\
&\qquad\qquad-\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{(N+1,...,M)\underline{\mathbf{c}}}{}^{1}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{N},\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{N+1}\cdots\mathcal{T}_{K})\mathcal{R}_{(N+1,...,M)\underline{\mathbf{c}}},\end{split}

thus proving the statement for
a\otimes b,c\otimes d,e\otimes f\in\left(\mathcal{A}^{(\geq 1)}(\mathcal{R})\bowtie_{\mathbf{r}}\mathcal{A}_{0}^{(\geq\ell-1)}\right)
. Note here that the results still hold if we substitute
\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}}\to 1
with
N>0
, and/or
\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{b}}}\to 1
with
M>N
, and/or
\mathcal{T}_{\underline{\mathbf{c}}}\to 1
with
K>M
, which then also proves the case where
a\otimes b
,
c\otimes d
and/or
e\otimes f
are elements of
\mathcal{A}_{0}
, thus also proving the lemma.
∎

We now have all the ingredients to prove Proposition
3.5
:

Proof for Proposition
3.5
.

Let us first write
\phi^{(1)}_{k|\ell}={}^{1}\phi^{(1)}_{k|\ell}+{}^{2}\phi^{(1)}_{k|\ell}+{}^{3}\phi^{(1)}_{k|\ell}
, where
{}^{i}\phi^{(1)}_{k|\ell}
is the Drinfeld associator corresponding to the twist
{}^{i}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}
as defined in (
156
). Next, let
a\otimes b,c\otimes d,e\otimes f\in\mathcal{A}_{0}\oplus\left(\mathcal{A}^{(\geq 1)}(\mathcal{R})\bowtie_{\mathbf{r}}\mathcal{A}_{0}^{(\geq\ell-1)}\right)
, then

\begin{split}{}^{i}\phi^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(a\otimes b,c\otimes d,e\otimes f)&=\varepsilon(a\cdot b)\ {}^{i}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(c\otimes d,e\otimes f)+{}^{i}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(a\otimes b,(c\otimes d)(e\otimes f))\\
&\quad-{}^{i}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}((a\otimes b)(c\otimes d),e\otimes f)-{}^{i}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(a\otimes b,c\otimes d)\varepsilon(e\cdot f)\end{split}

(162)

for
i=1,2,3
. Let us first consider
{}^{2}\phi^{(1)}_{k|\ell}
. From Lemma
A.4
, it follows that

\begin{split}&{}^{2}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(a\otimes b,(c\otimes d)(e\otimes f))=\sum\mathbf{r}^{-1}(d_{(1)},e_{(1)})\ {}^{2}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(a\otimes b,c\cdot e_{(2)}\otimes d_{(2)}\cdot f)\mathbf{r}(d_{(3)},e_{(3)})\\
&=\sum\mathbf{r}^{-1}(e_{(1)},f_{(1)})\gamma^{(1)}_{k}(a\otimes b,c\otimes d\cdot f_{(2)})\mathbf{r}(e_{(2)},f_{(3)})\\
&\quad+\sum\mathbf{r}^{-1}(d_{(1)},e_{(1)})\mathbf{r}^{-1}(b_{(1)},c_{(1)})\gamma^{(1)}_{k}(a\cdot c_{(2)}\otimes b_{(2)},e_{(2)}\otimes d_{(2)}\cdot f)\mathbf{r}(b_{(3)},c_{(3)})\mathbf{r}(d_{(3)},e_{(3)})\\
&={}^{2}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(a\otimes b,c\otimes d)\varepsilon(e\cdot f)\\
&+\sum\mathbf{r}^{-1}(d_{(1)},e_{(1)})\mathbf{r}^{-1}(b_{(1)},c_{(1)})\ {}^{2}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(a\cdot c_{(2)}\otimes b_{(2)},e_{(2)}\otimes d_{(2)}\cdot f)\mathbf{r}(b_{(3)},c_{(3)})\mathbf{r}(d_{(3)},e_{(3)}).\end{split}

where in the second equality we used that
\sum\mathbf{r}(e_{(1)},d_{(1)})\mathbf{r}(d_{(2)},e_{(2)})=\varepsilon(e)\varepsilon(d)
since
\mathbf{r}
is cotriangular. Similarly, we have

\begin{split}&{}^{2}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}((a\otimes b)(c\otimes d),e\otimes f)=\sum\mathbf{r}^{-1}(b_{(1)},c_{(1)})\ {}^{2}\gamma_{k|\ell}^{(1)}(a\cdot c_{(2)}\otimes b_{(2)}\cdot d,e\otimes f)\mathbf{r}(b_{(3)},c_{(3)})\\
&=\sum\mathbf{r}^{-1}(b_{(1)},c_{(1)})\mathbf{r}^{-1}(d_{(1)},e_{(1)})\ {}^{2}\gamma_{k|\ell}^{(1)}(a\cdot c_{(2)}\otimes b_{(2)},e_{(2)}\otimes d_{(2)}\cdot f)\mathbf{r}(d_{(3)},e_{(3)})\mathbf{r}(b_{(3)},c_{(3)})\\
&\quad+\sum\mathbf{r}^{-1}(a_{(1)},b_{(1)})\ {}^{2}\gamma_{k|\ell}^{(1)}(a_{(2)}\cdot c\otimes d,e\otimes f)\mathbf{r}(a_{(3)},b_{(3)}).\end{split}

Combining the terms now gives

\begin{split}{}^{2}\phi^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(a\otimes b,c\otimes d,e\otimes f)&=\varepsilon(a\cdot b)\ {}^{2}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(c\otimes d,e\otimes f)\\
&-\sum\mathbf{r}^{-1}(a_{(1)},b_{(1)})\ {}^{2}\gamma_{k|\ell}^{(1)}(a_{(2)}\cdot c\otimes d,e\otimes f)\mathbf{r}(a_{(3)},b_{(3)}).\end{split}

Similarly, it can be derived using Lemma
A.4
that

\begin{split}{}^{3}\phi^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(a\otimes b,c\otimes d,e\otimes f)&=\sum\mathbf{r}^{-1}(d_{(1)},e_{(1)})\ {}^{3}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(a\otimes b,c\cdot e_{(2)}\otimes d_{(2)})\varepsilon(f)\mathbf{r}(d_{(3)},e_{(3)})\\
&-{}^{3}\gamma^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(a\otimes b,c\otimes d)\varepsilon(e\cdot f).\end{split}

It now follows immediately from Lemma
A.5
that

\begin{split}\phi^{(1)}_{k|\ell}(a\otimes b,c\otimes d,e\otimes f)=\left({}^{1}\phi^{(1)}_{k|\ell}+{}^{2}\phi^{(1)}_{k|\ell}+{}^{3}\phi^{(1)}_{k|\ell}\right)(a\otimes b,c\otimes d,e\otimes f)=0,\end{split}

thus proving the statement.
∎

Appendix B
Relation to the
\Theta
-morphism

In this section, we will discuss the relation between the
\mathcal{B}[\mathbb{Q}_{3}]
long-range deformation and the
\Theta
-morphism
that was described in
48
. In that paper, the
\Theta
-morphism, which is a second-order derivative on the inhomogeneities of the spin chain, was used to derive the transfer matrix and the corresponding
N
-magnon states for the
B[\mathbb{Q}_{3}]
long-range deformation. Here, we will show that our transfer matrix derived from the deformed Lax operator is equal to the one found in that paper. Moreover, we then show that our deformed FRT-bialgebra contains operators that correspond to the
N
-magnon creation operators, by mapping these operators to the
N
-magnon states that were found in
48
. We emphasise here that we do not have full proofs for some of the claims we make. However, all claims have been verified explicitly in
Mathematica
. This section is therefore mainly for the illustrative purpose of identifying the correct
N
-magnon creation operators that would be needed in a proper long-range Algebraic Bethe Ansatz.

We consider the
R
-matrix corresponding to the Yangian
\mathcal{Y}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2})
, which is given by

\mathcal{R}_{12}(u,v)=\frac{u-v-\mathcal{P}_{12}}{u-v-1}\in\mathrm{End}(\mathbb{C}^{2}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{2})[\![u,v]\!],

(163)

which can be related to the more usual Lax operator for the XXX spin chain (see e.g.
42
) under the reparametrisation
\hat{u}=\frac{i}{2}-iu
and
\hat{v}=\frac{i}{2}-iv
. To make the relation between the
\mathcal{B}[\mathbb{Q}_{3}]
-deformed Lax operator as given in (
117
) and the
\Theta
-morphism, we will first rewrite the Lax operator in terms of differential operators. We will consider a non-trivial spectral parameter in coordinate
\underline{1}
, and trivial spectral parameters in the coordinates
2
,
3
. We then get

\begin{split}\mathcal{Q}^{(3)}_{3(\underline{1}2)}\mathcal{R}_{(\underline{1}2)3}&=\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{1}2}\mathcal{Q}^{(3)}_{3(2\underline{1})}\mathcal{R}_{(2\underline{1})3}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{1}2}=\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{1}2}\left(\mathcal{Q}^{(3)}_{2\underline{1}}+[\mathcal{Q}^{(2)}_{2\underline{1}},\mathcal{Q}^{(2)}_{32}]\right)\mathcal{R}_{\underline{1}(32)}\mathcal{R}_{23}\\
&=\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{1}2}\left(-\mathcal{R}_{\underline{1}2}\mathcal{Q}^{(3)}_{\underline{1}2}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{1}3}+\mathcal{Q}^{(2)}_{2\underline{1}}\mathcal{Q}^{(2)}_{32}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{1}(32)}-\mathcal{Q}^{(2)}_{32}\mathcal{Q}^{(2)}_{2\underline{1}}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{1}(32)}\right)\mathcal{R}_{23},\end{split}

(164)

where we used that
\mathcal{R}_{23}=\mathcal{P}_{23}
,
\mathcal{Q}^{(3)}_{23}=0
and
\mathcal{R}_{\underline{1}2}\mathcal{Q}^{(3)}_{\underline{1}2}=-\mathcal{Q}^{(3)}_{2\underline{1}}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{1}2}
, since
\mathcal{R}
is of difference form.
31
31

31

Indeed, from braiding unitarity
\mathcal{R}_{21}\mathcal{R}_{12}=1
and difference form
(\mathcal{D}_{1}+\mathcal{D}_{2})(\mathcal{R}_{12})=0
, it follows that
\mathcal{R}_{12}\mathcal{Q}^{(2)}_{12}=\mathcal{Q}^{(2)}_{21}\mathcal{R}_{12}
. By taking the derivative of this equation, the identity for
\mathcal{Q}^{(3)}_{12}
follows.
Next, from the definition of the third algebraic charge density, we have

\mathcal{Q}^{(3)}_{\underline{1}2}=-\left(\mathcal{Q}^{(2)}_{\underline{1}2}\right)^{2}+\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{1}2}\mathcal{D}_{\underline{1}}^{2}(\mathcal{R}_{\underline{1}2}),

(165)

and by the Sutherland equation, we have

\mathcal{Q}^{(2)}_{32}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{1}(32)}=\mathcal{R}_{\underline{1}(32)}\mathcal{Q}^{(2)}_{32}+\mathcal{R}_{\underline{1}2}\mathcal{Q}^{(2)}_{3\underline{1}}\mathcal{R}_{13}-\mathcal{Q}^{(2)}_{2\underline{1}}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{1}(32)}.

(166)

Using the fact that
\mathcal{R}
is of difference form such that
\mathcal{R}_{\underline{1}2}\mathcal{Q}^{(2)}_{\underline{1}2}=\mathcal{Q}^{(2)}_{2\underline{1}}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{1}2}
, we get

\begin{split}&\mathcal{Q}^{(3)}_{3(\underline{1}2)}\mathcal{R}_{(\underline{1}2)3}\\
&=\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{1}2}\left(-\mathcal{D}_{1}^{2}(\mathcal{R}_{\underline{1}2})\mathcal{R}_{\underline{1}3}+\mathcal{Q}^{(2)}_{2\underline{1}}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{1}2}\mathcal{Q}^{(3)}_{3\underline{1}}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{1}3}+\mathcal{Q}^{(2)}_{2\underline{1}}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{1}2}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{1}3}\mathcal{Q}^{(2)}_{32}-\mathcal{Q}^{(2)}_{32}\mathcal{Q}^{(2)}_{2\underline{1}}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{1}(32)}\right)\mathcal{R}_{23}\end{split}

(167)

Next, using
\mathcal{Q}^{(2)}_{2\underline{1}}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{1}2}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{1}3}=-[\mathcal{Q}^{(2)}_{32},\mathcal{R}_{\underline{1}2}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{1}3}]+\mathcal{R}_{\underline{1}2}\mathcal{Q}^{(2)}_{3\underline{1}}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{1}3}
, we get

\begin{split}\mathcal{Q}^{(3)}_{3(\underline{1}2)}\mathcal{R}_{(\underline{1}2)3}&=\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{1}2}\left(-\mathcal{D}_{1}^{2}(\mathcal{R}_{\underline{1}2})\mathcal{R}_{\underline{1}3}+\mathcal{Q}^{(2)}_{2\underline{1}}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{1}2}\mathcal{Q}^{(3)}_{3\underline{1}}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{1}3}-[\mathcal{Q}^{(2)}_{32},\mathcal{R}_{\underline{1}(32)}]\mathcal{Q}^{(2)}_{32}\right)\mathcal{R}_{23}\\
&+\mathcal{Q}^{(2)}_{3\underline{1}}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{1}3}\mathcal{Q}^{(2)}_{32}\mathcal{R}_{23}-\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{1}2}\mathcal{Q}^{(2)}_{32}\mathcal{Q}^{(2)}_{2\underline{1}}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{1}2}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{1}3}\mathcal{R}_{23}.\end{split}

(168)

Note now that
\mathcal{Q}^{(2)}_{\underline{2}1}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{1}2}=\mathcal{D}_{1}(\mathcal{R}_{\underline{1}2})=-\mathcal{D}_{2}(\mathcal{R}_{\underline{1}2})
since
\mathcal{R}
is of difference form, and where one should interpret the derivative in the second coordinate as
\mathcal{D}_{2}(\mathcal{R}_{\underline{1}2})=\frac{d}{dv}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{1}2}(u,v)\big|_{v=0}
, i.e. the derivative evaluated at a trivial spectral parameter. The above formula can now be completely written in terms of these kind of derivations. Namely, for some (matrix-valued) functions
g(v_{1},...,v_{n})\in\text{End}(V^{\otimes L})
, we define the operator

\xi_{n,m}(g)\coloneq\left(-\mathcal{D}_{m}^{2}g+\mathcal{D}_{n}\mathcal{D}_{m}g-[\mathfrak{q}^{(2)}_{nm},g]\mathfrak{q}^{(2)}_{nm}\right)\bigg|_{v_{i}=0},

(169)

where
\mathcal{D}_{i}
denotes the derivative in the
i
-th coordinate. Moreover, it can be calculated using
Mathematica
that there is a matrix
\mathfrak{m}
such that

\mathcal{Q}^{(2)}_{3\underline{1}}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{1}3}\mathcal{Q}^{(2)}_{32}\mathcal{R}_{23}-\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{1}2}\mathcal{Q}^{(2)}_{32}\mathcal{Q}^{(2)}_{2\underline{1}}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{1}2}\mathcal{R}_{\underline{1}3}\mathcal{R}_{23}=[\mathfrak{m}_{\underline{1}2},\mathcal{R}_{(\underline{1}2)3}].

(170)

Combining all the terms now gives that the Lax operator corresponding to the
\mathcal{B}[\mathbb{Q}_{3}]
deformation (
117
) is given by

\mathscr{L}^{\lambda}_{(\underline{\mathbf{a}}b)n}=\mathcal{R}_{(\underline{\mathbf{a}}b)n}+\lambda\left(\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}b}\xi_{nb}(\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}(nb)})\mathcal{R}_{bn}+[\mathfrak{m}_{\underline{a}b},\mathcal{R}_{(\underline{a}b)n}]\right)+\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{2}).

(171)

If we now calculate the monodromy matrix
\mathcal{T}^{\lambda}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}b}\coloneq\mathscr{L}^{\lambda}_{(\underline{\mathbf{a}}b)L}\cdots\mathscr{L}^{\lambda}_{(\underline{\mathbf{a}}b)1}
for
L\geq 2
, it follows that the first order correction term is given by

\begin{split}\mathcal{T}^{(1)}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}b}&=\sum_{n=1}^{L}\xi_{n,n+1}\left(\mathcal{T}^{(0)}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}}\right)\cdot\mathcal{T}^{(0)}_{b}+\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}b}\left[\mathcal{T}^{(0)}_{b}\cdot\xi_{L-1,L}\left(\mathcal{T}^{(0)}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}}\right)\right]\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}b}-\xi_{L,1}\left(\mathcal{T}^{(0)}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}}\right)\cdot\mathcal{T}^{(0)}_{b}\\
&+[\mathfrak{m}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}b},\mathcal{T}^{(0)}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}b}],\end{split}

(172)

where
\mathcal{T}^{(0)}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}}\coloneq\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}L}\cdots\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}1}
and
\mathcal{T}^{(0)}_{b}\coloneq\mathcal{P}_{bL}\cdots\mathcal{P}_{b1}
are the zero-th order terms of the monodromy matrix. This is a particularly nice closed-form formula completely in terms of the operator
\xi
as given in (
169
). Remember here that the above form only holds for
L\geq 2
, i.e. does not involve any wrapping effects. If we take the trace over the auxiliary space, it follows from the fact that
\mathrm{tr}_{b}(\mathcal{T}^{(0)}_{b})=\mathcal{U}
, with
\mathcal{U}
the shift operator, that only the first term in (
172
) survives. In particular, the transfer matrix is given by

\begin{split}t^{(1)}(u)\cdot\mathcal{U}^{-1}&\coloneq\mathrm{tr}_{\underline{a}b}\mathcal{T}^{(1)}_{\underline{a}b}(u)\cdot\mathcal{U}^{-1}=\sum_{n=1}^{L}\xi_{n,n+1}(t^{(0)}(u))\\
&=\sum_{n=1}^{L}\left(-\frac{1}{2}(\mathcal{D}_{n}-\mathcal{D}_{n+1})^{2}(t^{(0)}(u))-[\mathfrak{q}^{(2)}_{n,n+1},t^{(0)}(u)]\mathfrak{q}^{(2)}_{n,n+1}\right),\end{split}

(173)

where
t^{(0)}(u)
is the undeformed transfer matrix. One can now recognise the same form for the first order correction to the transfer matrix as was found in
(
48
, (6.25))
using the
\Theta
-morphism!

Next, we discuss the
N
-magnon creation operators for the long-range deformation. Here, we assume the reader is already familiar with the Algebraic Bethe Ansatz for the undeformed spin chain, see e.g.
41
;
85
for introductions. For the undeformed spin chain, the
N
-magnon creation operators are given by
B(u_{1})B(u_{2})\cdots B(u_{N})
, where
B(u_{i})\coloneq t_{12}(u_{i})
, which act the vacuum state
|\Omega\rangle\coloneq|{\downarrow}\rangle\otimes\cdots\otimes|{\downarrow}\rangle
. For the deformed spin chain, however, we have to take into account the doubling of the auxiliary space of the FRT-bialgebra. A natural generalisation, therefore, is that the
N
-magnon creation operators on the
\mathcal{B}[\mathbb{Q}_{3}]
-deformed spin chain are of the form
B(u_{1})\cdots B(u_{N})\otimes^{\lambda}\left[t_{11}(0)+f(u_{1},...,u_{N})t_{22}(0)\right]
for some symmetric function
f(u_{1},...,u_{N})
. Here, the notation
\otimes^{\lambda}
means that we view this tensor product as elements in the deformed double-crossed product algebra
(\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{R})\bowtie_{\mathbf{r}}\mathcal{A}_{0})(\gamma_{3})
. Indeed, since the
B(u_{i})
’s mutually commute, it is ensured that these operators are symmetric in the rapidities
u_{i}
. Moreover, an
N
-tuple of products of
B
’s also ensure that these create states with
N
spin-up excitations. By making the correspondence to the
\Theta
-morphism, we will show that the creation operators of this form are indeed the correct
N
-magnon creation operators.

We will denote the matrix elements of the monodromy matrix
\mathcal{T}_{\underline{a}b}^{\lambda}(u_{1},...,u_{N})
as given in (
172
) by
t_{i_{1},j_{1}}(u_{1})\cdots t_{i_{N},j_{N}}(u_{N})\otimes t_{k,\ell}(0)
, where we will also use the notation
A(u)\coloneq t_{11}(u)
,
B(u)\coloneq t_{12}(u)
,
C(u)\coloneq t_{21}(u)
and
D(u)\coloneq t_{22}(u)
. Recall that the coproduct of the
R
-matrix is given by

\mathcal{R}_{(\underline{a}_{1},...,\underline{a}_{N})b}=\mathcal{R}_{\underline{a}_{1},b}\cdots\mathcal{R}_{\underline{a}_{N},b}.

(174)

Applying this to the braiding
\mathcal{R}^{-1}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}b}\left[\mathcal{T}^{(0)}_{b}\cdot\xi_{L-1,L}\left(\mathcal{T}^{(0)}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}}\right)\right]\mathcal{R}_{\underline{\mathbf{a}}b}
, it can be calculated from (
172
) that the first order corrections to the operators acting on the spin chain are given by

\begin{split}B(u_{1})\cdots B(u_{N})\otimes^{(1)}t_{11}(0)&=\sum_{n=1}^{L-1}\xi_{n,n+1}(B(u_{1})\cdots B(u_{N}))A(0)\\
&+f^{-}_{0}(\mathbf{u})A(0)\xi_{L-1,L}(B(u_{1})\cdots B(u_{N}))\\
&+\sum_{i=1}^{N}f^{-}_{i}(\mathbf{u})B(0)\xi_{L-1,L}(B(u_{1})\cdots A(u_{i})\cdots B(u_{N}))+...,\end{split}

(175)

and

\begin{split}B(u_{1})\cdots B(u_{N})\otimes^{(1)}t_{22}(0)&=\sum_{n=1}^{L-1}\xi_{n,n+1}(B(u_{1})\cdots B(u_{N}))D(0)\\
&+f_{0}^{+}(\mathbf{u})D(0)\xi_{L-1,L}(B(u_{1})\cdots B(u_{N}))\\
&+\sum_{i=1}^{N}f_{i}^{+}(\mathbf{u})B(0)\xi_{L-1,L}(B(u_{1})\cdots D(u_{i})\cdots B(u_{N}))+...,\end{split}

(176)

where
A(u),D(u),B(u)
are the undeformed matrix elements
t_{11}(u),t_{22}(u)
and
t_{12}(u)
of the undeformed monodromy matrix
\mathcal{T}_{\underline{a}}(u)=\mathcal{R}_{\underline{a}L}(u)\cdots\mathcal{R}_{\underline{a}1}
, respectively, the functions
f_{i}^{\pm}
are given by

f_{i}^{\pm}(\mathbf{u})\coloneq f_{i}^{\pm}(u_{1},...,u_{N})=\begin{cases}\prod_{n=1}^{N}\frac{u_{n}}{u_{n}\pm 1}&\text{for }i=0,\\
\pm\frac{1}{u_{i}}\prod_{n=i}^{N}\frac{u_{n}}{u_{n}\pm 1}&\text{for }1\leq i\leq N,\end{cases}

(177)

and lastly the “
...
” in (
175
) and (
176
) include the terms corresponding to the similarity transformation by
\mathfrak{m}
of the monodromy matrix (
172
), which just corresponds to a change of basis of the algebra generators. We now want to combine (
175
) and (
176
) such that the
A(0)
and
D(0)
in the second terms add to the shift operator, and the
N
-magnon state will contain a term
\mathcal{U}\cdot\xi_{L-1,L}\sim\xi_{L,1}\cdot\mathcal{U}
. Therefore, we define the
N
-magnon state to be given by

|u_{1},...,u_{N}\rangle^{\lambda}\coloneq\left(B(u_{1})\cdots B(u_{N})\otimes^{\lambda}\left[t_{11}(0)+\frac{f^{-}_{0}(u_{1},...,u_{N})}{f^{+}_{0}(u_{1},...,u_{N})}t_{22}(0)\right]\right)|\Omega\rangle.

(178)

Note that this state is per construction symmetric in the rapidities
u_{1},...,u_{N}
and that it flips
N
down-spins into up-spins. Since
A(0)|\Omega\rangle=|\Omega\rangle
and
D(0)|\Omega\rangle=0
, it follows that the zero-th order term of this state is simply given by
|u_{1},...,u_{N}\rangle^{(0)}=B(u_{1})\cdots B(u_{N})|\Omega\rangle
, which is indeed the zero-th order
N
-magnon state when the rapidities
(u_{1},...,u_{N})
are on-shell, i.e. satisfy the Bethe equations

\left[\frac{\hat{u}_{n}+\frac{i}{2}}{\hat{u}_{n}-\frac{i}{2}}\right]^{L}=\prod_{m\neq n}\frac{\hat{u}_{n}-\hat{u}_{m}+i}{\hat{u}_{n}-\hat{u}_{m}-i},

(179)

for
\hat{u}_{n}\coloneq\frac{i}{2}-iu_{n}
. The first-order correction to this state is given by

\begin{split}|u_{1},...,u_{N}\rangle^{(1)}&=\sum_{n=1}^{L}\xi_{n,n+1}\left(B(u_{1})\cdots B(u_{N})\right)|\Omega\rangle+\text{extra terms},\end{split}

(180)

where the extra terms are given by

\begin{split}&\text{extra terms}\\
&=\bigg[f_{0}^{-}(u_{1},...,u_{N})\mathcal{U}\cdot\xi_{L-1,L}\left(B(u_{1})\cdots B(u_{N})\right)-\xi_{L,1}(B(u_{1})\cdots B(u_{N}))\bigg]|\Omega\rangle\\
&+\sum_{i=1}^{N}B(0)\cdot\xi_{L-1,L}\left(B(u_{1})\cdots\left[f^{-}_{i}(\mathbf{u})A(u_{i})+\frac{f_{0}^{-}(\mathbf{u})}{f_{0}^{+}(\mathbf{u})}f_{i}^{+}(\mathbf{u})D(u_{i})\right]\cdots B(u_{N})\right)|\Omega\rangle+....\end{split}

(181)

In (
180
), one can now see that the bulk term of the first-order correction to the
N
-magnon state is given by second-order derivatives in the inhomogeneities of the spin chain, similar to the
N
-magnon states that were derived in
48
. The (highly non-trivial) claim is now that, for
(u_{1},...,u_{N})
on-shell, there is a function
g(u_{1},...,u_{N})\in\mathbb{C}
such that the extra terms are given by

\text{extra terms}=\left[-\mathfrak{q}^{(2)}_{L,1}\cdot\mathbb{Q}_{2}+g(u_{1},...,u_{N})\right]|u_{1},...,u_{N}\rangle^{(0)}.

(182)

We remark that we do not have an explicit proof for this statement. However, it can be verified to be true using
Mathematica
(for small values of
L
and
N
). In particular, using the fact that
\mathfrak{q}^{(2)}_{n,n+1}|\Omega\rangle=0
, it then follows that the state (
178
), for
(u_{1},...,u_{N})
on-shell, is given by

|u_{1},...,u_{N}\rangle^{\lambda}=\left(1-\frac{\lambda}{2}\sum_{n=1}^{L}(\mathcal{D}_{n}-\mathcal{D}_{n+1})^{2}-\lambda\mathfrak{q}^{(2)}_{L,1}\cdot\mathbb{Q}_{2}+\lambda g(u_{1},...,u_{N})\right)(|u_{1},...,u_{N}\rangle^{(0)}),

(183)

where one can now recognise the exact same
N
-magnon state as was found in
(
48
, (6.23))
using the
\Theta
-morphism.
32
32

32

Note here that the extra factor of
g(u_{1},...,u_{N})
just corresponds to the rescaling of the state given by
|u_{1},...,u_{N}\rangle^{\lambda}\to(1+\lambda g(u_{1},...,u_{N}))|u_{1},...,u_{N}\rangle^{\lambda}+\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{2})
.
In that paper, it was shown that these states are indeed eigenvectors for the long-range transfer matrix for
L\geq 2
, i.e. without wrapping effects. In particular, this shows that the state (
178
) is indeed the correct
N
-magnon creation operator, which is expected to also be the creation operator when wrapping effects are included.
33
33

33

For the
\mathcal{B}[\mathbb{Q}_{3}]
deformation, this is a rather trivial statement, as wrapping only occurs on the spin chain of length
L=1
, for which the state (
178
) (almost) trivially is an eigenvector of the transfer matrix. For more general deformations, the creation operators are expected to be of similar forms, also on spin chains that include wrapping effects.
We emphasise again that we have not given any formal proof in our current discussion, as we bypassed some calculations using
Mathematica
. However, the important takeaway is that we are now able to identify the correct
N
-magnon creation operators in our algebra, which therefore brings us a small step closer to understanding the long-range Algebraic Bethe Ansatz. It is expected that the above discussion generalises to all the long-range deformations, and also to higher-order corrections.
